Polish was his territory, his republic. He came from a Jewish family, which is also very important, and became a master of Polish. Like his philosophical patrons, Bergson and Nietzsche, Bolesław Leśmian believed that every thing, every phenomenon or object exists in the continuous course of becoming. The contemporary works of Leśmian seemed to be a late echo of Young Poland, which is why they mostly silenced it. The Skamandrits actually rejected Lesmian's work in its entirety. They did not see its novelty and originality, which in time contributed to the recognition of Leśmian as the greatest poet of their time and one of the most important Polish 20th-century poets, whose poetry enjoys the interest of both literary researchers and readers. Bolesław Leśmian's creative achievements make us believe in the democratic role of literature, abolishing even the most expressive political and cultural divisions. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u10	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club regarding the parliamentary draft resolution on the commemoration of Bolesław Leśmian on the 140th anniversary of his birth and the 80th anniversary of his death. Bolesław Leśmian was born in Warsaw, in an intelligent family of Jewish origin. Classic high school and law school at the university. St. Vladimira graduated from Kiev. There he took a very active part in the artistic life of the Polish community, and youth spent in Ukraine influenced his work to a large extent. He debuts in 1895 with a poem of Sextyna, where he signs himself as Lesman, but since 1897 his poems have been signed with a polish name, Leśmian. In 1901, he arrives in Warsaw, but still remains on an artistic journey through Germany and France. In 1911 he co-founded an innovative Artistic Theatre in Warsaw. Leśmian considered poetry to be a kind of primal speech: the poet feels, recognizes and expresses the world, as the original man once did. He was fascinated by the thought of that era when the language was created and the reaction to reality was first and only. Leśmian wrote his poems in a way that every time he invented a new language. The poet considered the rhythm to be the most important feature of the poet's speech, as it reflects the rhythm of the world, and it positively affects the reader. In his 20's, his work was not yet appreciated. Real interest in Lesmian's poetry began in the early 1930s. He was then awarded a membership of the Polish Academy of Literature. He was called a language genius - he used it very freely. He was a man of great culture, connected old philosophies with contemporary skepticism. Bolesław Leśmian is a wonderful figure considered the most innovative, original and most extreme individuality of the beautiful 20th century literature. He was the creator of a new ballad type, the author of prose fairy tales. Polish genius was posthumously seen in it. The year 2017 is the 140th anniversary of the birthday and 80th anniversary of the death of Leśmian. The Civic Platform Parliamentary Club commends the work of the poet and will vote in favour of the resolution. Ladies and gentlemen, read Leśmian, but read with understanding. Thank you. [[Applause]]

Virtuos words - yes [[Game in the O.R., bell ringing.]] Very often we talk about Leśmian, a poet placed on an equal footing with Kochanowski, Mickiewicz or Słowacki. His work is so rich that until today it is still not a completely discovered land for the next generations of Poles, especially since his poetry did not fit into the main trends of the interwar period and he was on the sidelines of the literary world. This is a time when a poet learns Bergson's or Nietzsche's philosophy many times later as an inspiration for his work. His extremely vivid language, his extraordinary imagination, surprising metaphors or neologists, drew much from symbolism, but also from romanticism. Ballady presented in a modern way, using irony, paradoxes. He is the author of beautiful erotics, metaphysical poetry, in which he discussed art, nature, man. Leśmian was also the author of beautiful fairy tales for adults and children with oriental themes published in 1913 under the title "Sesame Klechdy." In 1933, he became a member of the Polish Academy of Literature. At the end of his life, Leśmian built in his poem a figure of Mr Błyszczyński, who created his own garden - a beautiful place rich in vegetation, a magical place, a place of dialogue with God. That is what Leśmian's poetry, which he has been creating throughout his life - an extremely rich, nature-like poetry that is present in his work. He took care of her and developed her all his life. The Kukiz Club15 fully supports the commemoration of Bolesław Leśmian's outstanding poet. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

When democracies want to impose the will of the majority on the general public, they can repress and silence the voices of enemies of conformism. The ruling majority can suppress unpopular forms of expression and often choose to do so, and then democratic societies become clearly enslaved. Democratic states need artists who challenge the tastes of the ruling majority. By resisting the power of the majority, the creators help to liberate themselves to society. Art is a form of freedom struggle. Art without democracy is possible, democracy without art - no. Well, if Leśmian had been among us today, he would have been a believer in such thinking. He was an outstanding poet, proselytist, playwright, literary critic. Of course, he came from a Jewish family. His works symbolize one of the most original phenomena of Polish 20th-century literature. He started his work in Russian, but later he chose Polish. The peak period of literary activity of Bolesław Leśmian falls on the interwar twentieth anniversary. Then the poetic volumes of "Ląka," "Shady drink" or "forest plot," which are richly derived from the tradition of Baroque, Romanticism and Young Poland appear. Bolesław Leśmian, like Jan Kochanowski, Adam Mickiewicz, Juliusz Słowacki, refers to folk and religious traditions, and his rich works and poetic imaginations find an outlet in rich vocabulary. The Club of the Polish People's Party supports the adoption of a resolution on the celebration of the 140th anniversary of the birthday and the 80th anniversary of the death of this outstanding Polish creator. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u32	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Act on cooperative savings and credit banks from print No. 1144 was the subject of the work of the Public Finance Commission on 11 January this year, when the first reading of the project took place. The aim of the project is to align the provisions of the current SKOK Act with the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 31 July 2015, which ruled that Article. The 60th SKOK Act of 5 November 2009 is incompatible with the Constitution because it does not differentiate between supervisory and corrective measures applied by the Financial Supervision Commission depending on the size of the cash register, i.e. in relation to large and small cash registers. Small cash registers operate to a lesser extent, on a smaller scale, less complicated and therefore poses less risk to themselves and to depositaries, to the members of the cash registers. The draft amendment introduces the definition of a small cooperative credit and savings bank, as well as the criteria, the conditions under which the money loses its status as a small cash register in which circumstances it loses it. The criterion for considering the cash register as small was the sum of the balance sheet assets at the end of the financial year, which must not exceed PLN 20 million, and the average annual number of members in the last financial year must not exceed PLN 10 000. persons. It also introduces the obligation to provide KNF with information on the fulfilment of the conditions and the loss of the status of the small cash register also for the planned next financial year. At the same time, the project prohibits the spending of electronic money by small money. A discussion took place during the committee meeting. Two applications were submitted for rejection which were not supported by the committee. The critical voices addressed to the project concerned the limitation of supervision by KNF of small cash registers, the criteria for recognising the cash register as small, mainly the number of members, the lack of ties. There were also suggestions that KNF was receiving control of the SKOKs, as well as comparing the situation of small banks in this respect to the situation of cooperative banks which are subject to greater rigor. It was also stressed that cashiers did not meet solvency standards at a level such as cooperative banks. And as usual, the opposition stressed the issue of insufficient public consultation. It follows from the justification of the draft law that the supervision of the Financial Supervision Commission over small cash registers will continue to be exercised. KNF will be able to carry out an analysis of the results of the audit activities carried out by the National SKOK Casa pursuant to Article. 63 Laws, methodical control concerning the rules for carrying out checks by the National Bank on the effectiveness and adequacy of their activities. There are also provisions in force that show that KNF control over small cash registers is not restricted, for example. Article 2 73 and other provisions of existing laws, which say e.g. about the possibility of introducing the commission board in cash registers. According to information from the Financial Supervision Commission, the amendment will now apply to nine banks with the largest balance sheet assets of PLN 18 million and about 2 000. members, i.e. as a result much smaller than the criteria adopted in the draft law. All these cash registers, which will cover the project, have a solvency ratio of 6,9 to 16,1, i.e. at a good level, so they are safe cash registers and, moreover, comparing them to cooperative banks in terms of capital adequacy, is not justified because cooperative banks are covered by CRD IV and SKOK-i have been excluded. In the course of the work, six amendments were tabled, which appeared in the project, among others. an amendment abolishing restrictions for cash registers on credit activities. These restrictions concerned credit activities only up to 3 years and for housing purposes up to 5 years in terms of the duration of lending. As a result of this amendment, these provisions have been eliminated, i.e. there are no such restrictions imposed on the cashiers proposed by the applicants. The aim was not to reduce the competitive capacity of these small banks above all, and besides the scale of their activities is not large enough to cause some turmoil in the financial market in Poland. We said it was nine cashiers, they're in good financial condition. The amendment made during the first reading also concerned the removal of changes excluding small cash from the possibility of entrusting intermediaries to businesses, including foreign entrepreneurs, in concluding contracts and changes concerning activities such as financial settlements or the conclusion of contracts, e.g. relating to insurance. In the light of the adoption of this amendment, such a right will still have small amounts of money. Another amendment clarified the provisions so that the Minister for Foreign Affairs. the financial institutions have adapted the statutory delegation concerning the scope and frequency of the cash register reports to the degree of complexity of their activities. But this does not only apply to reporting, but also to accounting rules, which the Minister's decision to do. financial institutions will be simplified for small cashiers. This right, or rather that obligation, is left for a period of 12 months from the date of entry into force of the Act. So when it comes to reporting or when it comes to simplifying accounting rules, the current rules are to apply for up to 12 months. Some provisions have also been introduced concerning the relationship between the cashier and the financial spokesman and the Ombudsman. insurance. The point is to determine precisely when the money can provide certain documents or data about its members without compromising professional secrecy. The fees for the Ombudsman's institution have also been reduced. As the turnover of the cash registers is negligible, their amount is limited to 0,0011% the amount of assets. The main article requested by the Constitutional Tribunal was Article. 60 talking about KNF supervision based on the SKOK Act. The same article. 60 was simply supplemented by a provision which says that supervision is to be appropriate to the complexity of the cashier's activities and the extent of the risks involved in the activity. There is a matching of not only supervisory measures but also remedies to which the Financial Supervision Commission is empowered to implement. The KNF's activities are based on the principle of proportionality, i.e. the use of milder measures, if there is no need for further measures. All six amendments tabled were accepted by the committee and, taking into account some of the legislative comments, 25 members of the committee supported the proposed solutions, three votes against, zero abstentions, and the draft was adopted by the committee. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u46	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarni Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Act on cooperative savings and credit banks. This project concerns the implementation of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 31 July 2015, sygn. Act K 41/12. The Constitutional Tribunal held that Article. The 60th Act of 5 July 2009 on cooperative savings and credit banks, in so far as it does not restrict the supervision measures of the Financial Supervision Commission over the activities of small banks, is incompatible with Article. 22 in conjunction with Article. Article 31 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3 and Article 4 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] shall apply mutatis mutandis. Article 58 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. This means that the Sejm should diversify the legal supervision measures of KNF over SKOKs due to the varying size of the cash registers and the extent of their lending and deposit activities. The applicants proposed a number of key amendments to the law on cooperative savings and credit banks. First of all, the definition of a small cash register was introduced in the dictionary due to the continued use of this concept in the draft law and the need to diversify the legal supervisory measures of KNF due to the varying size of the cash registers and the extent of their lending and deposit activities. The maximum level of the balance sheet total of the cashier was harmonised at a level less than the balance sheet total of the smallest cooperative bank, i.e. PLN 20.28 million. In addition, the provision sets the average annual number of members of the cash register at 10 000. persons. Due to numerous security requirements, the possibility of small electronic money registers being spent has been reduced. Thus, small cash registers were deprived of the possibility to spend electronic money. The prudential approach also reduced the possibility of entrusting a foreign trader or trader with the conclusion and amendment of contracts for the performance of activities and the performance of actual activities by means of an agency agreement. The draft law contains a provision which reflects the purpose of implementing the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal in the form of limiting the supervision measures of KNF over the activities of small cash registers by indicating that the supervision is carried out according to the complexity of the operations carried out by the cash registers and the extent of the risks involved in the activities of the cash registers. The Polish Bank Association has brought to the project many worthwhile remarks, which increase the safety of running cash registers and minimise the risk of deposit losses by members of credit and savings banks. It has been noted that the assets of PLN 20 million represent quite large volumes and that the average annual number of members of the cash register of PLN 10 000. people are far too high and need to be re-examined. In conclusion, the Association of Polish Banks stated that in a situation in which supervision of selected SKOKs was to be exercised once again by the National Bank and not by a public body, the Financial Supervision Commission, such SKOKs must be excluded from the deposit guarantee system of the Bank Guarantee Fund. The proposed solutions are, in our opinion, insufficient. The reform of this financial sector should go further. It could be, among other things. extend the maturity of outstanding claims to the size of the cooperative banks. SKOK-i is only 1% the market. It should be possible to develop cooperative savings and credit banks, not to reform de facto through the liquidation of the institution, because the provisions proposed in this law, apart from the fact that they are pursuing the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal, significantly limit the possibility of carrying out and developing the activities of cooperative savings and credit banks. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! So it seems very reasonable for these small financial actors to create such regulations and conditions as will allow them to cool down, turn into larger ones. We therefore leave here the power of the Financial Supervision Committee to interpret these terms and the scope of the action. The rapporteur answered Mrs Hennig-Kloski's question by 2 years in a situation where the scale of business will be increased. Because it is not prohibited to exchange shares, to include shares in other entities, whereas it is over-interpretation to use the schemes of mergers of these entities today in relation to small cash registers, because they do not participate in this action. These schemes that have been invoked may be carried out by larger entities, perhaps by the Stefczyk Kasa, but, please, let us not agree that operating in such models is dangerous, is something illegal, because many institutions providing insurance, insurance, banking and financial services operate on similar terms. As long as these rules are transparent and under the control of the law, they are entitled. Therefore, this type of initiative cannot be condemned a priori. The solvency ratio in these small cash registers is one of the highest and is definitely above the threshold which KNF defines as a minimum. I don't want to give you specific numbers because I don't feel entitled to it, but in the finance committee they've been presented to you. Mrs Skowrońska asks why this is not a government project. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u84	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present a report to the Committee on Public Finance on the draft law amending the Act on Public Offering and the Conditions for Introducing Financial Instruments to Organized Trading and on Public Companies. The Public Finance Commission, following its first reading and the examination of this draft law at its meeting on 24 January 2017, asks the High Sejm to approve the draft law submitted. The proposed bill proposes to amend the Act of 29 July 2005 on public offering and conditions for introducing financial instruments to organised trading and on public companies, commonly referred to as the Public Offering Act. The main objective of the project is to repeal the Article. 72 Act on Public Offering, which provides: Acquisition of shares of a public company in the number leading to an increase in the total number of votes by more than - in the first case - 10% the total number of votes in less than 60 days by an entity whose share in the total number of votes in that company is less than 33% , i - in the second case - 5% the total number of votes in less than 12 months, by a shareholder whose share in the total number of votes in that company is at least 33%, may only take place as a result of a call to sign up for the sale or conversion of those shares of not less than 10% respectively or 5% the total number of votes. The remaining amendments to the Act are the legislative consequence of this proposal. In Article 2, paragraph 2 is replaced by the following: Article 2 and Article 2 3 transitional provisions are recorded. The Act will contribute to a more efficient and competitive functioning of public companies, reduce the obligations imposed on capital market participants in Poland. At the committee meeting the draft was presented by Piotr Uściński, we listened to the positive position of the government and the Committee on Financial Supervision, a discussion was held. The Commission received an opinion from the Office for Sejm Analysis on the compatibility of the draft law with European Union law. Finally, on behalf of the Committee on Public Finance, I request the adoption of the bill presented. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u85	Thank you, sir. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Please take the floor of Mr Bartłomiej Stawiarski.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u86	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today we are dealing with the issue of the bill amending the Act on Public Offering and the Conditions of Introduction of Financial Instruments to Organized Trading and Public Companies, print Nos 1213 and 1223. Mr Bielecki, rapporteur of the Committee on Public Finance, just now spoke about these technical aspects of the proposed changes. In principle, these amendments result in the repeal of the obligation to announce the call referred to in Article. 72 of this law. Applicants indicate that they aim to take into account the current state of development of the Polish capital market. At the time of the regulation, the Polish capital market was undoubtedly at a different stage of development, and the call was intended to prevent buying stock of the public company from buying in short intervals. Such action could have resulted in the unnatural development of the share price of the company. At this stage of the development of the Polish capital market, the prohibition to purchase significant holdings of public companies through transactions concluded on a regulated market seems unreasonable. Applicants shall also stress that the resignation from the call referred to in Article. 72 of the Public Offering Act will not be equivalent to preventing, in quotes, the so-called. the silent acquisition of control over public companies. The shareholders are obliged to notify them of the acquisition of significant holdings of public companies. Of course, the Law and Justice Club takes the view that regulations or elements which are not already applicable in the current capital market or in other areas of life should be removed from legal trading. Support for the proposed changes is also justified, given the positive social and economic impacts described in the explanatory memorandum. It is reasonable to seek to reduce the obligations imposed on participants in the Polish capital market. Similarly, improving the efficiency and competitiveness of the takeover market in Poland must be assessed positively, especially since everything indicates that this will translate into an increase in the economic efficiency of public companies. The proposed regulations do not have a financial impact, which is not meaningless, but increase the transparency of the arrangements for taking over public companies. The transitional provisions on circumstantial and implementing acts have been ensured and a six-month time frame has been set for the entry into force of the new implementing rules. Therefore, the proposed regulation should be considered to be sound and have positive effects. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u104	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the draft law amending the Act on Public Offering and Conditions of Introduction of Financial Instruments to Organized Trading and Public Companies, print No. 1213. The essence of the proposal submitted, amending the Law of 29 July 2005, is to repeal the obligation to announce a call under Article. 72 laws. According to the applicants, this solution meets the needs of the development of the Polish capital market. The current obligation to announce the call was introduced at the time of the birth of the Polish capital market, which was at a different level of development than it is now. The purpose of this legal guarantee was to prevent the buying-in of shares of a public company in a short period of time, which could affect the course of the shares of the company in an unnatural way. The Free and Solidarni Circle shares the assessment of the socio-economic and legal and financial consequences presented in the explanatory memorandum of the bill. We recognise that this project will have a positive impact on the reduction of obligations imposed on participants in the Polish capital market. In addition, the proposed regulation will contribute to a more efficient and competitive capital investment market in the form of so-called capital investments. acquisitions in Poland and will translate into an increase in the economic efficiency of public companies by obtaining the benefits of increasing their activity and resulting reduction of their operating costs and thus contribute to strengthening the competitiveness of the national economy. We agree that the entry into force of the bill will not have financial consequences and that this legislative amendment will increase the transparency of the regulation on the acquisition of public companies. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Members of the Free and Solidarnia Circle will support this bill.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u108	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The opinion of the bill aims to introduce changes in the Polish legal system towards the compatibility of national rules on fees for patent agents with European Union law. In this respect, the proposal provides for the withdrawal of the minimum rates charged by patent advocates in contractual relations between the patent advocate and the customer. In the amended legal state, the minimum rates will remain only if the court or body is satisfied with the costs of substitution. The maintenance in the amended legal state of minimum rates for actions before the judicial authorities and the Patent Office will be informative. It should be noted that the European Commission issued its opinion in the February 2016 Decision. the case. In the absence of a reply from the Polish party or an unsatisfactory reply, the European Commission has the opportunity to refer the matter to the Court of Justice. The Commission accuses Poland that the fixing of minimum rates limits the free movement of service providers, as it constitutes a restriction for operators entering the market and is an obstacle to the free provision of services. It should be noted that giving up minimum rates and consequently reducing the costs of such services will increase the interest of traders in the patent process and in the protection of intellectual property. This can be a convenience and encouragement for small and medium-sized entrepreneurs. It should also be borne in mind that this does not affect the quality of the services provided. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u110	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The draft law amending the Law on Patent Advocates aims to regulate the application of minimum rates by patent Advocates. It arose because of reservations about these provisions notified by the European Commission. The introduction of the planned regulation will fully respond to the allegations and avoid referral to the Court of Justice of the European Union. On 25 February 2016, the European Commission issued an opinion addressed to the Republic of Poland on the minimum rates laid down in the Polish provisions for patent advocates. The Commission noted that setting minimum rates for patent agents limits the free movement of service providers as it constitutes a restriction for those who wish to enter the market but cannot compete in terms of prices for their services. Furthermore, it constitutes an obstacle to the freedom to provide services and hinders access to the services of the patent spokesperson. The application of minimum rates by patent attorneys is currently governed by the Regulation of the Minister of Justice of 2 December 2003 on fees for patent spokespersons. The proposal contained in the draft of this Act includes the waiver of the minimum rates charged by patent lawyers in the relationship between the patent attorney and the customer. In other words, minimum rates in customer relations - the spokesperson are abolished. Proposed amendment of the Article. The 13th Patent Advocates Act allows the application of minimum rates only for the purpose of establishing and judging the costs of substitution in proceedings before the judicial authorities and the Patent Office. Official rates will serve as an information function. The citizen, exercising his right to court and taking action before the judicial authorities and the Patent Office, will be aware of the order of expenditure he may expect. Of course, as is apparent from other provisions, in the event of actual costs lower than those laid down in the Regulation, reimbursement of actual costs will take place on the basis of the presented cost inventory and it is therefore possible to rule on the reimbursement of actual costs below the minimum rate. Changing the rules on minimum rates for patent advocates should be a significant convenience for entrepreneurs. It may result in lower patent costs, thereby increasing the availability of these services, in particular for micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. Developing the competitiveness segment of service providers, i.e. patent advocates, should increase the interest of entrepreneurs in patenting and patent protection of intellectual property. In view of the above, the Parliamentary Club of Law and Justice supports the draft law submitted. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u164	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! I have the honour to present a draft amendment to the Code of Administrative Procedure. This project is part of a package prepared by the Ministry of Development, so-called. a package of 100 changes for companies. Last year we worked on one of the laws, today there are three more projects in this package. In the beginning, I may point out that it is, of course, a government project, while in part this project also benefits from the work of the team, which was appointed by the President of the Supreme Administrative Court, which completed its work in 2016. Part of this work was also used in this project. K.p.a. it's a law about, I would say, a huge practical significance for citizens and offices. Hundreds, if not thousands, of citizens' affairs, including entrepreneurs, are settled in the administrative procedure per day. More than 80,000 are dealt with before administrative courts each year. complaints, of which as much as 20% - in favor of the parties. In my opinion, this is a very high percentage of cases that the administration actually loses in court. This shows that many cases are simply led by the administration in an improper way, i.e. errors, either substantive or procedural. In fact, the regulations that ensure the efficiency of the authorities and guarantee the protection of the legitimate rights of the parties are such an ideal, but also the foundation of modern administration - an administration that responds to the needs of citizens and at the same time protects the public interest. This is also the starting point for building citizens' confidence in the authorities themselves, which certainly also has an impact on the tendency to invest and respect for legal order. Yeah, that's right. this is so important, and at the same time in my opinion so far in the context of the discussion on improving the legal environment of entrepreneurs, an underappreciated legal act, if I may put it this way, even though almost every entrepreneur has to deal with these regulations on a daily basis. In order to improve the quality of administration activities, and really the comfort of serving citizens, including entrepreneurs, the project aims to solve three problems. First of all, the recognition of administrative matters is taking too long today. In a study conducted by PARP, as much as 78% entrepreneurs have indicated that this is an important problem for them. This concerns not only the duration of proceedings before individual authorities, but also the number of instances. This is due to an excessive number of so-called. casatorial decisions in relation to reformatory decisions. The same case can often be dealt with several times by the administrative authorities - once in the first instance, once in the second instance - before it finally goes to the administrative court. The reason, it seems, is also a model of procedure which assumes that before bringing an action against a decision before a court, it is necessary to bring an administrative action at all times. The same case is often dealt with several times by administrative authorities and courts. This also applies to cases where a decision is taken at first instance by central authorities, e.g. Minister, or self-government colleges of appeal. A party who is dissatisfied with the decision must submit a request for review by the same authority, although - and this is shown by the practice, the research chosen by us - the authorities very occasionally changed the decisions in this mode in a satisfactory manner of the addressee, the party. The conclusion is therefore, in fact, an action which does not lead to the settlement of the matter, merely a formal requirement to be fulfilled before the decision is brought before the court. Another problem - and I have already mentioned this - is the excess of casatorial decisions, that is, passing the case back to rediscovery. Notwithstanding the party's wish, the decision shall be revoked and the case referred to the body of first instance to be heard whenever it appears necessary in the appeal proceedings to investigate the material circumstances of the case. The procedures for questioning the decisions of the authorities are therefore currently insufficiently flexible and very formalised. In fact, today there is no way to adapt them to the real needs, the expectations of the participants in these proceedings. The second problem - it has actually already been discussed on this first aspect - is excessive formalism and very rigorously understood power in the application of administrative procedures. There is a lack of clear legal bases - and we know that administration works best when it has such a precise legal basis - to act on such more partnerly principles. Often, it is the practical functioning of a given authority that depends on whether the matter is dealt with more quickly or less slowly. The authorities also, acting formally, often fail to see the benefits that result from such an active involvement of the parties in the handling of the matter, and unfortunately, the obligation to inform about the facts and legal circumstances is purely formal. As a result, the party is surprised by the content of the decision and often does not understand the reasons for the decision being adopted by the authority. This in turn, of course, leads to appeal to subsequent instances. The third aspect to which we respond with this amendment is automation in the application of administrative penalties. This is a request, in science too, formulated for many years to put this issue in order. The current regulations generally do not allow the amount of the fine to be adjusted to the circumstances of a particular case. The authorities shall act in such a scheme: an infringement of the law - a fine in a rigid amount, or, if the penalty is determined by a quota range, a penalty which is most often punishable by that higher risk range. Therefore, penalties are often inadequately high in relation to the circumstances of the infringement of a particular provision. In many categories of cases there is a lack of rules that would allow the penalty to be fined or, because this should also be possible, will stop at instruction when the seriousness of the infringement is negligible. Again, such automation creates such a sense of social injustice and administrative penalties They are often more severe than criminal sanctions, which, I think, is a kind of paradox and not exactly a desirable situation. Therefore, if these three key or fundamental problems are taken into account - such as I mentioned - the project envisages solutions that will enable, firstly, speed up, streamlining administrative procedures, secondly, the creation of conditions for a more partnership between administration and citizens, which should in turn translate into a smaller scale of appeals and complaints to administrative courts, and, thirdly, ensuring that the administrative penalties applied are appropriate to infringements of the law. All these actions are part of the overall objective of increasing the efficiency of administration also in order to reduce administrative burden for citizens or even, more commonly speaking, bureaucratic mitręgi and to increase the sense that administration is from efficient, reliable but also fair handling of matters. Shortly defining the first objective within these three objectives of the solution, namely the smooth handling of administrative matters in order to improve the procedure, we propose, first of all, the possibility for the party to abandon those stages of the procedure which unduly and inefficiently extend it. We therefore assume that the party will be able to decide whether to take advantage of the request for review or to bring an action before the administrative court immediately, i.e. the request for review would be optional. We also propose that the party should have an impact on whether, if necessary, the case will be referred to the body of first instance for re-examination, or whether this additional investigation should be carried out by a second instance body, so that a final decision can be reached more quickly. As a general rule, if a party lodges a request, the authority will be required to make a reform decision according to this project. In addition, the project introduces a simplified way of challenging casatorial decisions - because without this mechanism the whole change will not be effective, i.e. it will not be effective - i.e. it will be possible to object to a casatorial decision and, contrary to the complaint, this objection will be recognised in the context of a summary procedure. The court will only assess whether the formal conditions for a casatorial decision are met, i.e. whether the appeal body was not in a position to resolve the matter in substance. In addition, in some sense we use the standards of the Civil Procedure Code, where such an institution worked, the court will be able to fine the authority, which should also reduce the number of cases too hastyly returned to the first instance by the appeal body. Nor will it be necessary to call on the authority to remedy the infringement before bringing an action before the administrative court against acts or acts for which the law does not provide for remedies, e.g. Resolutions of local authorities. Speeding up administrative procedures will also enable new, faster procedures for dealing with matters: so-called. Silent handling of the case and simplified procedure. We assume that these institutions will be applied in certain categories of cases which will be indicated in material laws. The project introduces such a model procedure of silence. We propose that the basic deadline for taking them out is a month. The failure of the authority to react within this time limit will result in the matter being dealt with as requested by the party. On the other hand, the simplified procedure is mainly dedicated to matters which are not factually and legally complicated. It shall restrict the investigation to the evidence submitted by the party to the request and the evidence at the disposal of the Authority. There are also strict requirements to justify the decision in this manner. Silent handling of the matter will also be a rule in the simplified procedure. A party who makes a complete application should therefore obtain a settlement within one month of the case being dealt with in that procedure. The introduction of such model procedures will be of great importance, in my opinion - systemic, because it will prevent the fragmentation of solutions within the institutions of administrative law, which are those institutions I am talking about, as is the case, for example. in the case of administrative penalties, where over the years the lack of general regulation has caused models - with regard to the way in which the law is created, i.e. in individual ministries - that each ministry has created its own premises, sometimes even in fact these issues have not been regulated at all. This can also be seen when trying to propose rules on penalties, how difficult it is today to find such common rules, so the introduction of such model institutions in the form of a silent settlement of the matter or a simplified procedure will, I think, serve to maintain greater coherence and logic of the rules to which it will be possible to refer in the material provisions. In addition to these solutions, we also propose simplifying formalities in cases where many parties participate. The draft provides that if more than 20 parties participate in the proceedings, it will be possible to notify them of the decision or other activities, e.g. the timing of the hearing, by posting information, for example, in the Public Information Bulletin of the Authority, provided, of course, that the parties have been given prior notice of such a form of notice. These solutions are complemented by the introduction of transparent rules to challenge inaction or the chronic conduct of proceedings with the use of urgency institutions. In order to improve this procedure, we propose a 7-day deadline to consider reminders and a few ordering changes, such as defining what inaction is. or chronicity. It is also practical to remove doubts as to the time when a complaint may be brought against the authorities for inactivity or chronicity. It will be an obligation to instruct the parties on the possibility of pressing if the proceedings are extended. At this second point, we expect that these solutions will contribute to a real shortening of administrative time. We know that, especially in the case of entrepreneurs, time is one of the most important assets of companies. The second objective - a partnership of administration towards citizens. Here, building openness and trust between the administration and the citizen requires changes in two basic areas. Firstly, which is an absolute novelty, so that it is possible to make greater use of amicable dispute resolution methods in administrative proceedings. Today we have such a procedure only in judicial proceedings. The aim is to shift the focus from the traditionally understood authority to the dialogue in the course of the settlement of the case, of course in accordance with the law. Mediation would have preceded the handling of the case. Secondly, in our view, it is necessary to amend and expand the catalogue of general principles, in particular to ensure that the ambiguities of rules or evidence collected do not have negative consequences for the parties. We propose that mediation should be allowed between the parties to the procedure and between the party and the body, and this is the element of novelty. First of all, this will allow for an amicable solution to the dispute between the parties, e.g. in neighbouring cases, it may be of great importance in complicated investment cases, but also in order for the authorities to explain more effectively to the parties all the circumstances of the case or to present certain aspects by the party itself to the body, which does not, of course, preclude the application of all other provisions, including an in-depth explanation of the factual and legal situation by the authority. Moreover, mediation is really one element of a larger whole, which we as the Ministry of Development are spreading here. We have changes in the creditor package that are conducive to this. I think that in a moment we will be going out with changes to the Labour Code, which in disputes between the employer and the employee will also spread mediation. This is also an initiative of the Social Dialogue Council, which we are taking together with the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy. It is also such an expression, I think, of a new approach to administration at all, but also administration to parties. Finally, we propose to modify the administrative settlement that has been in place for years so that the parties can reach out to it more often. On the other hand, as regards the general principles, here we are proposing, first of all, to introduce them into the c.p.a. principles of a friendly interpretation of the rules, ordering the resolution of factual doubts in favour of the party. Here, of course, we take the view that unclear rules or doubts about the facts should not have negative consequences for the party, for example, restrictions on its rights in the form of withdrawal of the permit. According to the project, in these cases this will be decided in favour of the party. The introduction of this principle to the c.p.a., I believe, is so important that in recent years we have such a strong tendency to transfer delinquency from the law of offences to administrative law and therefore these guarantees to the parties should be guaranteed to the same extent by administrative law as by the law of offences or criminal law. We propose to express it directly in the regulations of the C.P.A. the principle of legal certainty. In accordance with this principle, the Authority may waive established practice only if there are compelling reasons to do so. The aim is to eliminate such situations that, under the same circumstances, the body issues a different content to the different parties in the same legal state. We also propose supplementing the catalogue of general principles with the principle of proportionality, the principle of equality, the principle of the impartiality of bodies. The inspiration for adopting such rules is, among others. The European Code of Good Administrative Practice. All of these principles constitute a list of specific directives that authorities will have to follow in everyday life, most importantly, in practice, will be a source of responsibility for bodies on the one hand and, on the other hand, a real guarantee for the parties to the proceedings. Like I said, they're supposed to shape this new picture, a new culture of administrative work. Of course, we realize that before we know the full results, some of this time will have to go by. As far as the finals are concerned, I would like to say that the project also includes the concreteisation of information obligations, which should also increase the activity of the authorities in the proceedings and contribute again to the fact that there will be fewer appeals, i.e. more cases will be dealt with at first instance, because it will be the authority that will have to inform the party about what conditions it did not fulfil. Target three, in short, are administrative penalties. Here we have general rules for imposing them, granting reliefs, and even in certain situations the obligation to waive punishment. We hope that with these provisions penalties will be adequate to the extent of the infringement. Today it is different, there are such signals from different people, mainly from entrepreneurs, because it is the main recipient of conversations. They're disproportionate today, illogical. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In view of the above arguments, I request that this proposal be adopted for further parliamentary work. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u190	Mr. Marshal! Once again, I am standing before you, this time presenting a project that aims to facilitate the recovery of claims. Unfortunately, a large percentage of companies are still complaining about the problem of congestion caused by the late settlement of debts by counterparties. Of course, these effects are felt by micro, small and medium-sized enterprises. As far as industries are concerned, the construction industry and the transport industry are most affected. Thus, the State should create such legal infrastructure that creditors have effective legal instruments to counter this phenomenon both at the stage of redress before and after the stage of enforcement after judicial proceedings. Above all, however, here we are focusing our attention at the stage of entering into business relations with a partner or a potential partner who does not guarantee payment of this debt, i.e. at such a stage as to prevent delays in payments before the transaction takes place, so that we do not treat later, so as to really limit the consequences of non-payment. We provide for changes in 10 laws. We focus on these three main objectives. First of all, it is about allowing reliable and complete information on the reliability of the potential counterparty or already the debtor in order to allow the creditor to better assess the ability of that partner to settle new obligations, secondly, to streamline the investigation and to secure claims before the court. Thirdly, increasing the chances of debt enforcement. In order to achieve the first objective, we propose changes in the functioning of economic information offices and the creation of a Register of Public Claims. And as I said at the beginning, this is actually the main goal, I think, on which, you can say, this amendment or this project is focused. We assumed that time is actually crucial for recovery, and that the best chances are within the first 3 months of the maturity of the debt. Then they automatically fall down to 10%. , when this maturity is at least 3 years old. At that time, the level of recovery is already gradually lower and lower. With this philosophy, we wanted to create tools for a better assessment of the potential counterparty's ability to pay. In order to do so, economic information offices will be able to provide information to the person asking about the data collected in other offices as well. So the offices will have to exchange de facto these data. What discourages the use of these offices today is that each office needs to be performed separately. Offices will also have more information that they can share with the respondents. In addition, offices will be given the opportunity to create so-called. Predictive models, i.e. such analyses of future possible payment behaviours of the entrepreneur, because we do not apply this solution to consumers based on information collected by these offices. We also shorten the deadline after which you can declare your commitment to the office from 60 to 30 days, but at the same time we also pay great attention to ensuring the protection of the legitimate rights of debtors. And here there is a balance in this project, because we introduce rules for debtors to question the information submitted to the office, introduce an institution or propose to introduce an institution to object to the entry, but also after the entry already entered in the office, or update and delete entries, but also for example. a catalogue of circumstances which will be required by the office or creditor to take specific actions related to the entry made. The Office will also be required to inform the debtor of the acceptance of the obligation as expired. In addition, there will be a limited time limit on the possibility of transmitting information on consumer obligations to offices and disclosures and, finally, incorrect entries on the debtor will be regarded as an act of unfair competition or delict. So that I think that here these solutions have a balance in looking at the interests of the creditor, but also securing the legitimate rights of the debtor. And the Register of Public Claims. We enter a register where the entrepreneur, the citizen will be able to check the possible arrears towards the tax office or local government units which, as I will stress, are already subject to execution and the sum of which is at least 5 thousand, so that there will not be entered debts found to be illegal yet by decision, only those that are fit for execution and whose debtor has been prejudiced. The register will be kept in the IT system by the head of the National Tax Administration. The data, which is also important, will not be made public, they will only be available to the questioner. We also provide, of course, legal remedies: the debtor's opposition to an irregular entry in the register. Thus, with this additional information, we increase the safety of economic turnover. And, I don't know, it can be interesting information in the context of the assessment of this solution also that the inspiration for its preparation together with the Ministry of Finance, because it was prepared in this way, were the demands of the entrepreneurs themselves, who said that: what I know that X is behind with Y's company, or rather: it doesn't fail, since if this company has certain tax claims towards the Treasury, then I know that it will not pay me, because most debtors, however, primarily regulate public debt. Thus, this is important information for the creditor if his potential counterparty does not pay public debt. So that, as I say, this is a request from entrepreneurs themselves who are having trouble assessing the reliability of their counterparties.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u210	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The project aims to introduce into Polish criminal law material and procedural, as well as implementing tools to improve the effectiveness, effectiveness of depriving criminals of the benefits of criminal offences. The purpose of the Act is to implement Directive 2014/42 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on the freezing and confiscation of tools for committing crime and the benefits of crime in the European Union. In principle, this project concerns five points. First of all, it extends the so-called. the extended infringement - not only the direct benefit of the offence, but also the indirect benefit, extends the possibility of a criminal offence without prior conviction in the event of redemption or suspension of proceedings, introduces provisions on the loss of an undertaking as a specific instrument for committing the offence, will safeguard the continuity of the firm's life in the event of a security of the imminent loss (so-called "the case'). the compulsory management), and finally introduces effective tools for the search for and disclosure of property at risk of loss. The obligation to introduce the possibility of forfeiture without conviction arises directly from Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Directive, which I have just mentioned, and will apply to perpetrators of significant value and to other serious offences at risk of imprisonment, the upper limit of which is not less than 5 years. The perpetrator will have to prove the legality of the property acquired within five years before the crime was committed. In answer to all kinds of questions and doubts about the relationship between the proposed rules and the presumption of innocence or the principle of protection of property, I would like to point out that these solutions already exist in at least 15 countries, among others. in the UK, Spain, Ireland, Italy, the Netherlands, France, Germany, Belgium. They have been subject to the evaluation of the European Court of Human Rights on several occasions and the Court has found that the Convention on the Protection of Human Rights and Fundamental Freedoms has not been infringed. Confiscation without conviction is possible in case of remission for reasons of formal proceedings for serious crime, as well as suspension of proceedings due to illness or the escape of the perpetrator. This is a fundamental novelty, namely that we are introducing a detachment in the shape of what the European Court of Human Rights shows, as well as a directive... There is a break in the bond between liability and the legality of the origin of the property. The property, the way in which this property is acquired and ruled, has nothing to do with the question of the defendant's criminal liability for his alleged act. The obligation to introduce the possibility of forfeiture comes directly from the directive, as I have pointed out, and this confiscation has been examined on several occasions by the European Court of Human Rights, which has not seen a breach of human and civil rights standards. The company's confiscation, the new designed tool, is used to bring justice to criminals who pretend to be entrepreneurs, but in fact they are unfair business people. And this dishonesty is qualified, i.e. here it is not enough to demonstrate a lack of special care, a lack of care, but it must be a concrete intention, and it must be a direct intention or a possible intention, linked to the involvement of the trader in the criminal activity carried out by a third party. This solution is limited only to enterprises owned by a natural person. The actions of criminals, as the practice shows, are detrimental not only to the Treasury, but above all to legitimate businesses or citizens, that I will mention only fraud in connection with VAT invoices or parabanking activities. The purpose of the regulation is not to take over the management of undertakings, but to deprive the perpetrator of the instrument of committing the offence or its benefits. The perpetrator often does not have any assets formally, which limits the effectiveness of criminal sanctions against him, and despite his conviction, he still has the opportunity to benefit from this asset. This property is the basis for its further criminal activity, it continues to do so thanks to this property, and therefore it is necessary to cut it absolutely and basically from these criminal roots. Then an important element is the confiscation of the company - as I mentioned - belonging to a third party. Namely, if the owner or any other person empowered by not only failing to observe the precautions required under the circumstances, but also the specific intention of a possible undertaking were to agree or directly wish that the undertaking was intended to commit the offence, then such confiscation is an option. This solution is limited only, as I mentioned, to companies owned by a natural person. They should be expected to maintain such standards as I mentioned. If someone wants or agrees to have a third party use his company for criminal activity, then there is absolutely no reason for this company to continue serving that owner so that he can continue to have it and continue to use it. The solutions proposed are absolutely far from any automation in judgment. First of all, the security issues are characterized by the fact that we raise the standard in relation to the general security mode. Namely, it's not a prosecutor, but the court will decide in case of company security. Implementation - as I have just said - of the canons designated by the European Court of Human Rights, as well as the EU directive obliges us to introduce such solutions. Let me just briefly mention some indirect solutions that are regulated by the way. The Act also introduces changes in the field of operational control, namely imposing an obligation on law enforcement authorities, in particular on the Police, to disclose property at risk of loss as one of the objectives of operational and research activities and extends the scope of operational control by the Police - among others. By the police, criminal offences against the justice system, so that the confiscation of the offence will take place as a result of an earlier analysis of criminal activities as a result of the prior work of criminal groups. Therefore, such additional changes are also necessary. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u214	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed draft law contained in Seym printing No 1186 refers to an extremely important but also complex matter, which is to deprive the perpetrators of the benefits of criminal offences. It is obvious - and the Civic Platform Club also stands in this position - that it is necessary to pursue and punish all criminals with all determination. Consequently, these criminals must also be deprived of assets that have been illegally collected. The starting point for the adoption of the proposed bill is not only to strengthen legal mechanisms to improve the efficiency of prosecution of economic offences, but also to implement the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014. In view of the need to adopt new regulations, however, I would like to point out that the bill presented raises many controversy and reservations. Firstly, it contains a number of legal and legislative defects, and secondly, introduces new powers for law enforcement apparatus and special services, which will give the possibility of surveillance of Polish entrepreneurs. One of the most important changes is the proposal to add an article in the Penal Code. 44a, which is intended to serve as a basis for the use of the so-called. the confiscation of an enlarged one. However, it raises legal doubts, which was raised at the stage of work on the project. It is extremely important that the new regulations are consistent and harmonious with the existing legal system. The Ministry of Justice proposes that the court should be able to rule on the forfeiture of an undertaking owned by the perpetrator. Unfortunately, the provision does not refer to the definition of a company as defined by civil law. There is therefore a real risk of prosecutors applying different assessment criteria from those currently in force, what is the company and what is part of it. Such a state of uncertainty will undoubtedly have a negative impact on the atmosphere of conducting business activity in Poland. The same provision also lacks a clear indication as to the extent to which the company may be forfeited, namely whether the court will always rule on the whole or whether it will be able to rule on only part of the company. This is extremely important in the context of the huge diversity of possible components of the company, such as machinery, personnel and accounting documentation, but also know-how and information covered by trade secrets. The proposal for a new wording also raises huge reservations. Article 2 45 criminal code. It implies breaking up with one of the fundamental principles of criminal law, namely the principle of presumption of innocence. In fact, a catalogue of cases is introduced, when, by virtue of the law, there will be an assumption that the property collected by someone is an illegal property. Such a person will have to prove that she acquired her assets on a legal basis. After all, not every citizen collects at home for 5 years any evidence of making purchases, even if they are valuable items. So if someone has been convicted of a crime and has obtained a property benefit, then it cannot be assumed that everything he has acquired in the previous five years is illegal. As a result, however, the absence of a receipt or any proof of purchase may make someone a criminal. Proposals for amendments to the Code of Criminal Procedure regarding the rules governing the protection of assets are also extremely important. The Ministry of Justice proposes to introduce a new way of securing the execution of a future court ruling by establishing a compulsory management board of the company and designating the administrator. At the stage of the pre-trial procedure, the decision on such safeguards will be issued by the prosecutor and, after the prosecution, by the competent court. Let me just remind you that, in its original assumptions, this order issued by the prosecutor was not to be subject to judicial review. However, the public voices of opposition raised by Members of the Civic Platform, but above all by a wide range of experts, have resulted in the final consideration of judicial review. This does not change the fact that this new provision continues to cause a number of controversy. about its subsequent interpretation. As I mentioned at the outset, the bill also extends the powers of law enforcement and special services in the field of so-called. Operational and Recognizing Functions. The amendments proposed by the Ministry of Justice will concern the police, the Central Anti-corruption Office, the Military Police Department, the Border Guard, the Military Counterintelligence Service and the Military Intelligence Service. I would just like to remind you that the persons responsible for supervising these services are now convicted by the court of abuse of power and then pardoned by President Andrzej Duda. So he can't surprise anyone by the fact that [[♪ The bell ♪]] Each time special services are granted new powers...

These provisions concerned, in particular, the penalisation of the possession of criminal assets alone or the introduction of restrictions on the possibility of a decision on a forfeiture in respect of persons who have not committed a crime. New solutions should also be noted concerning issues related to the protection of the company's losses by establishing a compulsory management board. It has already been said here that this procedure was originally not subject to judicial review, it is now, but also under this procedure, it is possible to secure a collective undertaking, which is no longer so obvious. Also with regard to the security of this property, the current proposed rules do not contain time-limits which would determine the duration of the compulsory management. Unfortunately, the current bill also contains a number of solutions which have not been subject to any public consultation and concern important issues related to civil rights and freedoms. It is unacceptable to allow operational control to practically all uniformed services, and this is only for the disclosure of assets at stake. This leads to a significant extension of the catalogue of offences subject to operational control and, consequently, to practically universal surveillance against citizens and, above all, entrepreneurs, including entrepreneurs, those who are not subject to criminal proceedings. Reference only in those provisions to the acts referred to in Article. 45 § 2 Penal Code or Art. 32 § 2 of the Tax Code does not constitute any restriction on the application of operational control, as it is precisely these provisions that allow the transfer of property in respect of a third party, thus the circle of entities potentially subject to surveillance remains even unlimited. Such regulations raise fundamental concerns about their compliance with Article. 8 the European Convention on Human Rights and Article. 47 Constitution of the Republic of Poland, i.e. violations of the right to privacy and protection of family life. The proposed solutions also address a significant extension of the powers of uniformed services related to access to legally protected information, in particular the powers of the police, which will have access to data protected by different kinds of secrecy without any control by an independent authority, which the Ombudsman rightly points out in his opinions. It should be stressed that the proposed provisions on the possibility of a company being forfeited infringe the principle of proportionality, i.e. the preservation of constitutional grounds for interference with property rights. The standard Article is unacceptable. 45 § 2 resulting in the transfer to the accused, but also to third parties, of the burden of proof that the assets acquired within 5 years prior to the conviction were not derived from the offence. Such a construction is primarily in breach of the principle of presumption of innocence as the basis for the functioning of democratic states. I know you're the Minister. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u239	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party Allow me to present our position on the draft law amending the Penal Code Act and some other laws contained in print no. 1186. The aim of this project is to introduce into Polish criminal law the so-called "material, procedural and implementing institution." confiscation of the extended and, at the same time, implementing the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on the direction, it is difficult not to share the arguments that this would be an institution needed and indicated. But what else is the goal, and what else, Minister, is the means to achieve this goal. Unfortunately, the analysis of the project in terms of individual tools for the effective implementation of this institution, and not only in line with the principle that Mr Milewski of the PiS spoke of, in accordance with the Bible's principle: steal not, but I will also mention, an omen, a biblical principle to be guided by law and justice... One must be in connection with the other, and unfortunately the analysis of this project shows that not in all records it goes hand in hand. For example, in the proposed article. 44a § 5 There is a reservation that the company's loss cannot be determined if the damage caused by the offence or the value of the hidden advantage is not significant in relation to the size of the company's activities. Such a solution does not seem sufficient to safeguard the application of the principle of proportionality of the strict sense when it is possible to rule on the loss of an undertaking. The draft article also raises doubts. 48a of the Penal Code, which introduces the possibility of judgment in cases where the court would not apply the offence for the reasons set out in the draft Article. 44a § 4 and 6 if this would be disproportionate to the seriousness of the offence committed, the degree of culpability of the accused or the motivation and manner of conduct of the owner of the undertaking, if the damage caused by the offence or the value of the hidden advantage is not significant in relation to the size of the undertaking's activities and in other particularly justified cases where it would be disproportionate to the owner of the undertaking. This design potentially allows for a judgment of a criminal measure against a person who is not even subject to criminal proceedings, e.g. the owner of the third party undertaking. It cannot be accepted from the perspective of the right to a fair trial, one of which is the principle that it cannot be ruled twice in the same case. The loss of property of persons who are not even being dealt with gives rise to great opportunities for abuse. Those third parties are also effectively deprived of their rights of defence in the full sense of the word. It also raises concerns about the possibility of setting up a guarantee on the company against a possible future loss by introducing a compulsory board of directors. In particular, the wording of the article is unclear. 292b k.p.k. introducing the possibility of a security on an undertaking belonging to a collective entity, while the proposed Article. 44a § 1 and 2 k.k.a. points out clearly that the company is only possible if it belongs to a natural person. So, what do you say, the state of the applicant? Is the transfer of property also intended to concern collective entities or individuals alone? You will create an unclear situation here, there is an internal contradiction in the project. In accordance with the proposed Article. 45 § 2 The criminal code in case of conviction of the perpetrator any property which he acquired within 5 years prior to the judgment is to be treated as an advantage derived from the offence and is to be forfeited. It should be made clear that this provision and its proposed temporal scope are incompatible with constitutional and international standards of presumption of innocence. From where the authors took these 5 years, it is difficult to guess. In view of the above, the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party makes support for this project conditional on the removal of these controversial entries. Mr. Marshal, thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u241	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The aim of this draft law is to improve the effectiveness of actions that deprive criminals of the property benefits they have obtained as a result of the crime. However, although the intention of the designers is correct, the solutions contained in the amendment may raise many reservations. Recovering property derived from criminal offences is an important part of the fight against crime. To date, the range of opportunities that economic criminals have to exploit to avoid this is very wide. There is no doubt, therefore, that measures to improve the effectiveness of measures to reduce their financial impunity are necessary. The project proposes a number of changes to meet the initial assumptions of the project promoters. Among them there are among others. the extension of the catalogue of offences for which a transfer of property may be ordered, to offences from which the perpetrator has obtained, directly or indirectly, a property benefit and which are punishable by a maximum penalty of not less than 5 years. I consider it appropriate to burden the criminal with the need to prove the legality of the source of the property at risk of loss, which he obtained within five years before he committed a proven crime. I agree with the argument that in the case of crimes committed by organized groups or criminals who make themselves a permanent source of income, it should be presumed that the crime for which they are tried is not the only one they committed. Importantly, the new regulations also provide for the possibility of forfeiture of assets which the perpetrator has transferred to third parties free of charge or for an amount lower than the market in order to avoid confiscation, and allow for the loss of a non-culprit enterprise, but for which there are grounds to consider it as a tool for the offence. The proposed changes are alleged to infringe property rights and the freedom of economic activity. However, I believe that the lack of sharpening in this area will contribute to further escalation of the problem. Those who violate the law in order to obtain property benefits after their judgments often return to their luxurious homes bought with money from crime. With an adequate amount of funds from previously committed illegal activities, they continue their illegal activities, which is evidence of the ineffectiveness of the functioning system. Given that these restrictions have already been applied in many European countries, I see no reason why they would not apply in Poland either. About Free and Solidarni will vote in favour of further proceding this project. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u247	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! There have been many allegations of compliance with the presumption of innocence. In two words, I will only refer to the discussion that took place.
