Moreover, last year we have a directive on the presumption of innocence and in the justification for the proposal for a directive we clearly read - the proposal is made by the European Commission: the presumption of innocence states that the burden of proof lies with the prosecution, and any question of guilt must be settled in favour of suspects or accused persons. In dubio pro reo. So that it is clearly said about wine. There is absolutely no question of property, possession of this property. It's about guilt. So that's first of all. Therefore, at the outset, I reject all the doubts that have arisen here in the context of the alleged contradiction with the presumption of innocence. This has absolutely no connection, and I will point to four judgments of the European Court of Human Rights in order to answer the second question. I apologize immediately, Madam, but I will reply to you earlier. The issue of death was examined by the European Court of Human Rights in the case of Silicone v Lithuania. It was a situation where the wife of a high officer in the Lithuanian Treasury and his mother lost their assets as a result of confiscation, and he was the one who was accused and died during the proceedings. They challenged this decision by the Lithuanian authorities, the Lithuanian courts to the European Court of Human Rights and the widow of the accused lost. The Court did not see any evidence of a presumption of innocence. He absolutely stated that the confiscation of property after the deceased, in the event of confiscation of property belonging to a third party - after all, here the property of the wife was confiscated, and the property of the accused's mother - is as much in line with the European Convention. This is the first of the relevant judgments. The second ruling, which was groundbreaking, concerned the Phillips case against the United Kingdom, which was about assuming the criminal origin of the property 6 years ago. The Court also concluded that this is absolutely necessary, necessary and the presumption of innocence or property does not prejudice this. And what's more, it's logical. Why? Because that's our argument, that is, criminal activity is just a frame from a movie that's going on. We don't know if a criminal, a gangster, was acquired a year ago, two years ago, three years ago. We're keeping him red-handed right now, and we don't know under what circumstances and when he acquired it. That's why we're presuming that he bought it five years ago. In other countries, in Bulgaria, these periods are many times longer. In the UK, this is a 6-year period, which was examined by the Court. The Court found compliance with the Convention. We introduced an even shorter period: 5 years. Another ruling, very important to our project. Van Offeren v Netherlands. This was about the situation where the court acquitted the perpetrator of the drug trade, but the perpetrator was unable to indicate where his property came from - the entire property was lost. Even though he was acquitted from drug trafficking, his inability to identify the source of his assets put him at risk of losing his assets. The case, I would say, as many others in the European Court. Such, I would say, is standard for this type of case law as regards the question of the protection of property rights or the presumption of innocence. So that these examples must be shown, given and shown how far away we are from European standards. None of the countries of the European Union would have allowed themselves to do what our country has done in recent years. And thank you for that question, Madam Member. The country recovers 1% property in relation to that which loses as a result of criminal activity - 1%. Last year it was just 200 million PLN. So that a good yacht is worth almost 1/4 of this price. We have absolutely no tools to fight this kind of crime. From 2004 to 2014, the number of organised criminal groups in Poland has increased several times. The increase in the activity of these groups in the last years is 129%. I will also point only to the multi-billion-dollar losses of the State Treasury, which were almost equal to the budget deficit, but only to VAT crime. So that we end with the helplessness of the state, we end with the theoretical state and the kind of solutions that have been in place for many other cases for many years. She drew attention to 1990. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u249	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The proposed law amends the Act of 27 April 2001. Environmental law regarding the appointment of selected bodies of the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management and of the Regional Environmental and Water Management Funds, aimed at reducing the cost of their operation, simplifying and speeding up procedures for the appointment of their members and, as a result, improving the work of these bodies. The reduction in the operating costs of selected fund bodies is intended to amend the Article. 400d paragraph. 1 - reduction in the number of members of the national supervisory board of the fund, Article. 400f paragraph. 1 - reduction in the number of members of the supervisory boards of voivodship funds and Article. 400 lips. 1 - reduction of the number of members of the management boards of the voivodship funds. The simplification and acceleration of the procedures for the appointment of members of supervisory boards shall be aimed at amending Article. 400f bill. At the moment, the procedure for setting up the 7-person supervisory boards of the Funds is inconsistent, complex, long-term and in practice leads to situations which may raise significant doubts, because the legislator has granted powers to appoint and dismiss members of the supervisory boards of the funds to the Sejm of voivodships and, at the same time, to the minister responsible for environmental protection who dismisses the members of the Supervisory Boards of the Funds if they no longer fulfil the functions set out in the Act. Moreover, the law in the article. 400f paragraph. 2 provides that the supervisory boards of the Funds shall consist of persons with specific functions, persons notified by a seymist from among certain categories of candidates and persons notified or designated by certain entities. The process of notification and designation is complicated, and since it is complemented by the establishment of a voivodship seymik, there is an unnecessary time gap between these activities. The shortcomings of the current solution are particularly evident in the case of withdrawal of the notification or withdrawal of the designation of the person concerned who is part of the supervisory board of the fund and at the same time the notification or appointment of a new person. In such a situation, a change in the composition of the supervisory board of the fund may take up to 3 months in the doubts raised as to the legitimacy of a member of the supervisory board of the fund whose notification or designation has been withdrawn. The proposed solution for granting the Minister responsible for the environment competence to appoint and dismiss the members of the supervisory boards of the Funds therefore introduces one coherent and consistent solution which allows for a smooth, without unnecessary delays, change in the composition of the supervisory boards of the voivodship funds. The aim of the solution of granting the minister responsible for the environment competence to appoint members of councils not designated by the voivodship and the management board of the national environmental fund or the sejm of the voivodship of the representatives of the minister is to discipline the entities in question as to the smooth appointment of those members and to enable the supervisory boards of the funds to function effectively. The solution introduced in the new paragraph shall be similar with regard to the appointment of the management boards of the Funds. Article 2a 400th Act according to which, if the Management Board of the voivodship does not appoint members of the Management Board of the Fund within 7 days in accordance with the proposal of the Supervisory Board of the Fund, the appointment of members of the Management Board of the Fund shall be carried out by the Supervisory Board. The proposed amendment is to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication in the Official Journal of the Laws. The transitional provisions contained in the project will allow for smooth changes in the above-mentioned compositions. bodies of funds without disturbing their current work. The project is compatible with European Union law. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u251	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I recommend to you a parliamentary project concerning amendments to the Environmental Law Act, contained in print No 1127, in the section on composition and procedure of appointment of members of supervisory boards and national and voivodship boards of environmental and water management funds. The procedure for appointment of members of the supervisory boards of environmental funds is to be simplified and their composition is limited from 12 to 11 persons in the case of the national environmental fund and in the case of voivodship funds from seven to five persons. The number of management boards of voivodship funds is to be reduced from four to one, up to two persons. The National Environmental Fund and the Voivodship Environmental Funds are environmental institutions established by the Environmental Law, and indeed a State-owned special-purpose fund designed to finance public-funded tasks, which were considered so important that it was decided to finance them under a separate structure. A similar fund is e.g. Labour Fund, Social Insurance Fund or State Fund for the Rehabilitation of Persons with Disabilities. In the current state of law, regional environmental directors or their deputies, regional environmental inspectors, the chairman of regional nature conservation councils, the chairman of the environmental committees of the provincial Sejmists, the director of the Departments of Environmental Protection of Marshals' Offices, and finally representatives of environmental organizations, sat in the supervisory boards of the regional funds. In other words, the supervisory boards included the same persons who perform environmental functions in different institutions, which is definitely not a good solution. Now a few sentences on changes in the membership of the management board of the funds. There is no obstacle to the management of even a large company or a public company being carried out on a one-man basis, although in practice, e.g. in various types of companies, it is that it is carried out by two persons. In principle, there is no need for the boards of public companies to be multi-membered in the event of activities exceeding the scope of the ordinary board next to the President to be signed by a procurator. The same applies to voivodship environmental funds, where, in the case of a single-member board, the signature may be signed alongside the president of the fund, which is in line with the current arrangements. In other words, currently existing multi-member boards, even five-members, are a solution that is not possible even in large public companies. Five may be a board of directors, not a board of directors. In fact, the practice shows that this is an optimal number. The reduction in the number and supervision of managers of environmental funds is therefore aimed not only at savings related to the costs of unnecessary multiplication of positions in these bodies, but, above all, at improving their performance. A similar objective is to simplify the appointment of members of the management boards and supervisory boards of the Funds. Of course, at this point, the opposition will call for the refusal of the possibility of the members of the supervisory boards to be appointed by the Provincial Sejm to be a further attempt on democracy in the exercise of the Law and Justice. So why can't it affect the cast of posts in such an important place that it doesn't say the key structure for the functioning of this resort, which is the voivodship environmental funds? Incidentally, since, on the basis of current legislation, the minister responsible for environmental matters has the power to dismiss members of supervisory boards, it is illogical that he is deprived of his powers to appoint them. The current draft amendment to the Environmental Law simplifies and rationalises both the procedures for the appointment of management boards and members of the supervisory boards of voivodship environmental funds, and aims to improve the functioning of these bodies. Therefore, on behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I would like to ask you and your fellow Members to accept the changes and solutions recommended here. Thank you for your attention. The chairman of the Regional Nature Councils or the chairman of the Regional Environmental Impact Assessment Commissions will be mentioned as members of the Boards appointed by the Management Board of the National Environmental Protection and Water Management Fund. The chairman of the committee responsible for the environment of the voivodships will be listed as members of the councils appointed by the voivodship. The councils will be completely eliminated by environmental directors of marshal offices and representatives of environmental organisations notified by organisations operating and holding organizational structures in the region with the support of the largest number of such organisations. This project aims to eliminate from the supervisory function of independent environmental organisations, which are the sole in the eye of the governing party and in this case the Minister of Szyszka. The only representative of the local government remains the Vice-President of the Supervisory Board. There is no denying that changes in voivodship funds lead to the centralisation of the environmental financing system and its submission to government administration. The Supervisory Board shall be composed of a representative of the national fund. Self-governments will no longer have any impact on what environmental projects will be co-financed. The project is concerned that the needs of local communities will no longer be met, because so far the funds have been independent of the government and have dealt with environmental problems in their backyard regardless of the policy direction chosen by the government and the Ministry of the Environment. The proposed amendment aims to take away this independence. Art. 15 of the Constitution refers to the principle of decentralisation of public authority, which includes local government as a form of participation of citizens in the rule of rule about the ability to perform public tasks with a clear guarantee of independence in their implementation. The proposed changes violate this constitutional principle, because in a democratic country we should not seek centralised administration. Art. Article 16 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 the Constitution speaks of public tasks carried out by the local government on behalf of itself and focused on meeting the needs of the inhabitants, including the tasks contracted, as laid down in the laws in accordance with the principle of proportionality. According to art. 165 of the Constitution and the case-law of the Constitutional Tribunal The legal personality of local self-government units is based on local self-government in local and other matters provided by the laws. The proposed changes are an interference in the independence of a local legal person, which is a regional environmental protection fund. The proposed personal exchange for persons recommended by the government administration is a violation of the independence of the voivodship funds. The project is so dangerous and opposed by local governments that the projecters have been through the consultation stage, which was possible thanks to the fact that it is a draft of the group of Members of Law and Justice. In summary, the law is incompatible with the Polish constitution, restricts the role and competence of the local government, its impact on local investments in the field of ecology, another institution completely subordinates the government administration, which is directly the governing party, is another staff law, which is why the Poselski Club of Modernity is applying to reject its project entirely in its first reading. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u306	Thank you, sir. The discussion is closed. A motion to reject the draft law at first reading was put forward in the discussion. We will proceed to the vote on this proposal in the voting block. We proceed to the examination of item 10 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law on amending the Law on Measurements and the Law on voivodeship and government administration in the voivodship (print No 1195). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Development of Mr Mariusz Haladyj to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u307	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present today the third project with its first reading, and which is part of the so-called. a package of 100 changes for companies prepared by the Ministry of Development. This time it is a draft amendment to the Law on Measurements, which includes both changes in the organisation of the Main Office of Measurements and changes in certain procedures. Let me make this brief. Generally speaking, metrology is a science about measurements and their applications, which - I think it can be said - is somewhat underestimated, and which is actually unnoticed. He's with us every day. Measurements, and this is important in terms of precisely the objectives of this revision, are at the heart of technological development in almost all areas of the modern economy and enable it to function efficiently and develop. The aim of metrology is primarily to ensure fairness and certainty of economic turnover, and with the development of industry - also technical and production security, but also to stimulate innovation. For example, measurement methods are now widely used in, for example, analyses on climate change or carbon emissions. In all developed countries, a special unit is responsible for maintaining and managing the national measurement system. With us this function is performed by the Main Office of Measurements, whose main objective is to ensure measurement consistency for measurements carried out in the area of economy, science and social life. Today's office structure is headquarters, nine district offices and 58 regional offices. But what is also important from the point of view of this amendment is that GUM is not only an office, but these are primarily laboratories of metrology plants with scientists working in them. And this is the resource and potential that should be better used for the economy, because GUM as a national metrology unit should serve the development of Polish industry. This potential, in our opinion, is also confirmed in conversations or after numerous consultations with the industry environment, is not fully exploited and these solutions, which are today, are not fully suited to the challenges posed by the Metrology industry, the modern economy. Hence the need to extend the GUM's catalogue of activities and changes in its structure in order to actually adapt the functioning of this office to the challenges of the economy. This is related, for example, to changes that occur in computer science, robotics, automation or the development of nanotechnology. These phenomena are now facing completely new challenges for metrology, and new areas are developing, where metrology has a very important role to play, such as. pharmacy, biotechnology, medicine, food safety, energy, environmental protection, combating climate change, information exchange technologies, satellite techniques, protection of citizens' privacy, protection against terrorism, new military technologies and even the issue of anti-doping research. Therefore, in fact, metrology should be, in some ways, a forge of new technological processes, but with a very wide application. Here, too, we can draw attention to the correlation between metrology, technical regulations and innovation. Technical regulations have this positive impact on innovation, which is confirmed by scientific research, and the greatest benefits for innovation are when they give businesses a great deal of freedom to choose the measures they can achieve to meet the requirements of technical law in the form of general product quality parameters. or technological processes. This is how innovation in technology is created, but also in organisational solutions. An element, or even - I would say even - the basis of many such works in turn are measurement operations, because without them it is often impossible to reach optimal solutions. The consultations that we conducted in both the scientific and industrial environment together with the Main Office of Measurements have shown the need to change the tasks and activities of the Main Office of Measurements and to adapt them precisely to the challenges of the innovative economy. Here the amendment focuses primarily on the introduction of key tasks of the President for the development of innovation tasks related to research, development, supporting, inspiring the development of national industry through technology transfer. These tasks will be carried out, in particular, through consultation, technical advice on instruments, methods, measurement technologies, organisation and training, as well as research on measurement and measurement technologies. In this way, technical solutions needed by industry to implement a specific product or technology will also be discussed. For example, we are now discussing the introduction in Poland of a system for dynamic measurement of vehicle mass in motion, which is important from the point of view of GDDKiA or ITD. And just to play a central role in this work will be imposed on the Main Office of Measures from the point of view of carrying out various kinds of tests and studies. Therefore, the imposition on GUM of these obligations and, on the other hand, competence whether the powers will make it possible to say, perhaps a little simpler, Polish companies, various kinds of industrial companies, laboratories, scientific institutions will again obtain in the Polish country a partner in the form of an institution which has access to unique technological knowledge and whose statutory task will be already to support industry in various forms tailored to its needs. And here GUM will be obliged to share its know-how with industry, to respond to its needs, to initiate joint projects, for example through European programmes. Until now, the activities of the GUM in this area have been limited and, in the case of cooperation with the industry, even trace. This is the main objective that we have here in order to change the profile of the Main Office of Measurements. In order to plan our business, we also introduce strategic planning into the office - it is important that it is closely correlated with the objectives of the Polish economy, with the interests of industrial enterprises. The GUM will establish a Metrology Council which will be responsible for the substantive supervision of the GUM. What is important, the Council will focus in equal proportions representatives of industry, science and state institutions. In this respect, entrepreneurs will also have an impact on the functioning of the Main Office of Measurements and the evaluation of its functioning, and the Council will be given specific tools in this regard. The implementation of the long-standing demands of industrial companies, in particular those which operate on a mass scale, is also an opportunity to use the so-called ones more widely than today. statistical validation. This is, you can say, a deregulation change, because the current method is to check each individual unit, each instrument and, of course, it is time-consuming, cost-consuming, requires the involvement of significant resources. We want to allow more use of the statistical method, which is the method according to which the test is subjected to a representative sample taken at random from a given batch of measuring instruments. This will, of course, reduce the costs of measurements mainly borne by companies. In the project we also propose provisions that should help to address the problem of incorrect use of measuring instruments. Here, I think the loudest thing is measuring instruments in the form of meters at fuel stations. There's a lot of irregularities found here by NIK, for example. It is also about rationalisation, because we introduce or propose to introduce an institution so-called. temporary withdrawal of instruments from the market where they do not comply with certain requirements. There is no such temporary withdrawal from the market today, and therefore this device is permanently withdrawn, because restoring it, so to speak, is much more complicated to the market. Here there will be a time limit for such freezing of an instrument in order to renew the validity of these tests and so that the trader can market the instrument more quickly. There are also some changes in the sealing of the control system, such as sanctions. Today is so - it seems, curiosity, it should be changed - that with acts of involuntary and deliberate, there is an identical threat of punishment. The project also provides for flattening the field structure of the measurement administration by abolishing the peripheral measurement offices and setting up local branches in their place. This also helps users, especially entrepreneurs, to make access to measuring services easier, because today, let's say, there is a kind of connection between an entrepreneur and a given peripheral measurement office, which causes, if the individual peripheral measurement offices do not have a similar burden, that some entrepreneurs can wait weeks to carry out legalization studies and at another office the potential is simply untapped. Therefore, here entrepreneurs will have much more flexible access to GUM field units. In conclusion, the amendment is intended to make the national industry in the form of a Polish office, the Main Office of Measures, a partner who, as I said, has access to unique technological knowledge and whose statutory task will now be to support industry in various forms. We hope that this will also enable entrepreneurs to gain efficient and flexible measures, also more effective market surveillance, because this is also the task of the office - to ensure fair competition between entrepreneurs. Statistical validation will reduce costs, reduce bureaucratic Mitra. I think that this will benefit primarily from entrepreneurs involved in the transmission of media such as gas, water and energy. The proposed changes mean benefits for entrepreneurs, but above all, the implementation of particularly industrial, but also scientific demands to increase the role of GUM in the field of technology transfer to industry. The technical requirements, as I said at the beginning, for the various areas of the economy, whether environmental or safety-related, which are precisely defined in the framework, are one of the most effective incentives for innovation. Technological progress offers the opportunity to reduce administrative or control burdens. So, on the one hand, it is driving progress, but progress also makes it possible to mitigate administrative and control burdens on companies later. On behalf of the Council of Ministers, I ask you to refer the draft amendment to further parliamentary work. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u309	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the government draft law amending the Law on Measurements and the Law on voivodeship and government administration in voivodship, print No 1195. The main task of metrology, i.e. the science of measurement methods and the principles of interpretation of the results obtained, is to ensure, as the Minister has already said here, the integrity and certainty of economic turnover, and with the development of industry also technical and production security. The rapid and energetic technological development of the modern economy means that metrology must also change. These are growing new areas of the economy in which metrology is applied and which could not grow intensively without it. This is linked, on the one hand, to the general tendency to create smaller and smaller products, and on the other, to the development of techniques that enable man to create various structures, even at the level of individual atoms and molecules. This creates a situation where measurement technologies are at the heart of development in almost all areas of the modern economy. With the change in the role of metrology in the economy and social life, the functions of the state associated with this metrology are also changing. In the case of measuring instruments, the verification and approval of the types of measuring equipment and the entire legal metrology control, which has so far been the substance of the public authority in this area, shall be replaced by tests of compliance with international standards carried out by market operators or by the manufacturer himself. On the other hand, the role of the State is focused on investing in R &amp; D projects and technology transfer in order to promote the competitiveness of the national industry. The legislative changes proposed by the government aim at a number of different tasks. First of all, they aim to clarify the legal nature of the verification institution, to improve the carrying out of legal metrology checks at the place of installation or use of the measuring instrument, to eliminate interpretation doubts, to enable the re-certification of measuring instruments legally placed on the market and used not only as a result of conformity assessment, and finally to introduce the possibility of re-certification of a homogeneous batch of measuring instruments so-called. the statistical method and its definition. In addition, the project envisages simplifying the field structure of the measurement administration by transforming existing regional offices into local districts. The number of out-of-town departments is not expected to change in relation to the number of existing out-of-town offices. The current three-level structure with narrow local characteristics makes it difficult to use existing resources of measurement administration flexibly and efficiently and complicates decision-making processes. This is because at the moment there are as many as 58 circuits, each of which should work formally only in the area of its properties. In addition, the Act provides for the establishment of a long-standing Metrology Council which would bring together experts working with the Main Office of Measurements. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice is in favour of sending the project to the committee for further work. Thank you very much.

The project has been consulted with the measurement bodies, institutions, manufacturers, market operators and users of these instruments and economic organisations representing the abovementioned operators. The objective is to reform the Main Office of Measurements. It is a fact that the institution is to support the development of the national industry and to conduct and coordinate research and development in the area of measurement technologies. After the amendment, the Main Office of Measures is to become a partner for entrepreneurs. Here I will quote some words: He will share his know-how and access to unique technological knowledge. The Main Office of Measures is expected to respond to the needs of industry and initiate joint projects with it. Sounds decent. Let's hope that's really how it's done. The proposed amendment also provides for the appointment of a Metrology Council at the President of the Main Office of Measurements. The task of the board will be to evaluate the state and direction of development of the Polish metrology, the activity of the substantive administration of measurements, the four-year strategic plan of the Main Office of Measurements and the annual action plans of the Main Office of Measurements and the reports on their implementation. The Council is also to have the right to review the documentation of the measurement administration and to participate in the selection process of the President of the Main Office of Measurements. The members of the Council are to be appointed by the Minister responsible for Economic Affairs. It will be composed of representatives of science and representatives of state institutions involved in metrology issues. Here, too, we see another basis for creating new jobs. The draft law also provides for the reform of the structure of the field administrations of measurements. The regional offices will be transformed into the local departments of the district offices. I read: The new solution will allow for more efficient and flexible management of infrastructure, human and financial resources within the district structure, i.e. the possibility of certain specialisation of local departments and the temporary posting of workers to control economic operators operating in other departments, according to the actual need for metrology activities. Are you sure? Isn't this about the HR exchange? The amendment is also intended to introduce the possibility of a wider use of the so-called. statistical validation, i.e. selective submission of control counters, also at the verification of re-entry. This is the implementation of the long-standing demand of industrial companies to ensure that in certain areas where mass-scale measuring devices are put on the market, this is particularly true for media such as gas, water, energy and demand. The draft law also proposes provisions to address the problem of incorrect use of measuring instruments, which concerns the temporary withdrawal of the measuring instrument from the market and the increase of sanctions for infringements of the law. Many issues are actually the answer to our expectations, and therefore Modern is in favour of directing the project to work in committee. [[Applause]]

And today just every instrument in the whole series is controlled one by one, which is obviously not the most effective method of control nowadays - these are the changes that we adopted in November and which entered into force on 1 January. Please note that we are moving all inspections and all control authorities to a risk analysis check just to increase the efficiency of these services and, on the one hand, to benefit, of course, from the public interest, but on the other hand, to reduce the control burden associated with the operation of companies that do not pose a risk of generating any irregularities, and to focus on those areas where this risk is greater. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u331	Dear Marshal, High Seym! The proposed amendments to the Law on the Law on the System of General Courts are designed to organise the system of operation of the directors of courts in order to improve the efficiency of staff management in common courts with regard to the positions of directors and their alternates. In particular, they concern the rules governing the appointment and dismissal of the directors of the courts and their alternates, the rules governing the duties of the directors of the courts, as well as the taking by the Presidents of the courts of activities with financial implications. The draft provides for a clear separation between the functions of judicial administration falling within the competence of the President of the Court in the performance of judicial tasks and administrative activities falling within the competence of the Director of the Court. The draft introduces changes to the service liability of the Director of the Court. According to this project, the head of the court's director will be the Minister of Justice, while the President of the court will perform labour law activities in relation to the head of the court, except, of course, those reserved for the Minister of Justice. In line with the proposed draft arrangements, the appointment and dismissal of the directors of the courts and the deputy directors of the courts will take place on the basis of the establishment of the Labour Code. Importantly, High Sejm, the requirements of the candidates for the post of director and deputy director of the court have been respected. Therefore, as has been the case so far, these will be high criteria, high requirements to be met to apply for such a position. With regard to the appointment of deputy directors of the courts, the principle is maintained that appointment to that position is possible if special considerations so warrant, including the size of the court and the size of its tasks. The High Seym, the system we're dealing with today, is malfunctioning. I think that you yourselves have been able to determine this by observing recently what was happening in the courts, whether in Krakow or in Wrocław, and the courts responsible for it. If we do not learn from this, we can ask the question: What will we learn from? In addition - it is no secret - for years one of the most serious problems affecting the Polish justice system is the chronicity of court proceedings. In particular, when judicial representatives are asked what this chronicity comes from, it is often the answer that judges have too many tasks, which cause them to be pulled away from case law. It's a recipe. It means that we are now talking straight: the President of the Court will no longer have to take an interest in administrative, financial, and budgeted by the Minister of Finance in the area, and he will be able to focus on case law, which is what he is called for to do. So it's very clear. That is: management of funds and current administrative matters - the director of the court, that is his task; case law and other previous tasks - the President of the court. There you go, now the presidents of the court will have more time to deal with what they cared so much about. That is why this project organizes many organisational and formal issues in the current functioning of the institution, which are common courts. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u339	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Modern requests that the bill be rejected at first reading. Why? Firstly, the removal of the Director of the status of a judicial authority and the transfer of all powers to the Director under the Minister of Justice lead in fact to the loss of financial independence by the courts. This violates the constitutional principle of judicial independence from other authorities. Secondly, the proposed amendment, which provides that the Director loses the status of the body, entails the deprivation of independence and responsibility for the decisions taken. Thirdly, the appointment of directors without a competition violates the Constitution as it is not possible for applicants to participate in a transparent competition, the appointment depends only on the discretionary decision of the Minister, on which there is no appeal. Fourthly, the withdrawal of the Director without any justification should not take place at all. The appeal shall indicate the substantive grounds. Already in 2013, the Constitutional Tribunal considered that the general assemblies of the judges of appeal and the Presidents of the court should have a significant impact on the appeal of the director of the court. Fifthly, the act of the President not included in the financial plan requires the prior approval of the Director, which aims to limit his competence and authority arising from the employment relationship, e.g. in terms of motivating workers to better work. Sixthly, the proposed amendment poses a risk to the performance by judges of the statutory obligation to improve professional qualifications, e.g. because of the lack of training without the approval of the Director, and in fact without the Minister's consent. After seven, the Director's loss of the status of the body will lead to the loss of his power to represent the court before the labour court in the event of a dispute with the employees. This poses a threat to the division of competences and responsibility. For example, the President will represent the court in a dispute with the employee to the extent that it falls within the competence of the Director. It is worrying to say that the Presidents will be able to submit to a greater degree... to stress the tasks of supervising judicial proceedings. Why? Because, according to the Constitution, the Presidents have no control over the sphere of judgments and should not have it. They should not have it in a democratic state of law. It is not true that the proposed changes will affect the efficiency of the courts, because imagine: the President must ask the Director and the Director of the Minister of Justice. The fundamental change in this respect is a new definition of the status of directors of the courts, whose authority will be the Minister of Justice. This leads to the fact that the head of the court will no longer be one of the judicial authorities in regional, district and appeal courts. Surely a precisely formulated provision of the law setting out such a catalogue of activities reserved for the Minister would reassure those who are seeking an attempt on the independence of the courts in the proposed change, and would make it clear that the aim of the project is only to supervise the minister's administrative activities of the courts. The draft also envisages that the competition procedure laid down in the provisions of the Article will not be applied to the appointment of the directors of the courts. Article 32a (2) (13) of the amended law. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u361	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The current law of 24 January 1991 on combatants and certain victims of war and post-war repression, OJ No. U. z 2016 poz. 1255, does not regulate cases concerning the uniforming of veterans. The purpose of the amendments proposed in the government bill, print No 1167, is to emphasize that the Polish State recognises and honors members of the Veterans' Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland, among others. soldiers cursed, insurgents of Warsaw, soldiers of the Polish Armed Forces in the West, as soldiers of the Polish Army by granting the right to receive and wear uniforms along with signs of military degrees. The granted law will be an expression of appreciation for their steadfast military attitude and merit in the fight for independence of the Republic of Poland. The proposed amendment of the law will allow the Minister of National Defence to determine the uniform pattern of the members of the Veterans Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland. The provisions implementing the Act will also regulate the circumstances and manner of wearing uniforms and elements thereof. It is proposed to use the definition of uniform instead of uniform because of the need to distinguish such clothes from those used by the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland uniforms. In addition, it is proposed to introduce provisions specifying the person competent to issue the uniform to which the application is to be made and the elements of the application and the types of documents required to accompany the application, including. a copy of the supporting document of the military degree held. The project also proposes to extend the catalogue of entities informed by the Head of the Office for Combatants and Persons Repressed of the fact that the person concerned is deprived of his/her veteran rights to the authority competent to issue the uniform of the Veterans' Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland in order to ensure that the unauthorized person does not receive the uniform. It should also be stated that the fact that the veteran rights are denied will be clear with the refusal of the right to wear uniforms as a result of the wording of the proposed Article. 20, according to which the right to wear uniform has a member of the Veteran Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland. The costs associated with the implementation of uniforms will be financed from the budget of the State, part of which the Minister for National Defence is responsible. The estimated unit cost of making uniforms is about PLN 3800. Currently 8615 people are members of the Veteran Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland. Based on consultation with the Kompatan environments, the number of people who are likely to apply for uniforms should be estimated at around 3,000. persons. The draft provides that the Act will enter into force 14 days after the date of publication. The National Defence Commission authorised the Legal Office of the Polish Sejm to introduce editorial amendments to the draft. Following the first reading, the National Defence Committee unanimously adopted this draft law. The National Defence Committee asks the High Sejm to adopt the attached draft law contained in print no. 1203. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u372	Thank you very much. Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u373	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In the proposed amendment to the law on combatants and certain victims of war repression and the post-war period of 24 January 1991, the applicant's intention is to restore the form of special honoring of heroes by modeling the actions taken by the independent Republic in 1919 against the last surviving January insurgents. The aim of the proposed regulation is to highlight the special recognition and recognition by the state of Polish members of the Veteran Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland, among others. soldiers cursed, insurgents of Warsaw, soldiers of the Polish Armed Forces in the West, as soldiers of the Polish Army by granting them the right to receive and wear uniforms along with signs of military degrees. The granted law will be an expression of appreciation for their steadfast military attitude and merit in the fight for independence of the Republic of Poland and will highlight the continuity of the traditions of soldiers of generations. At the same time, it is proposed to use the definition of uniform instead of uniforming because it is necessary to distinguish such clothing from uniforms used by the Armed Forces of the Republic of Poland, because according to the law the uniform is a garment or parts thereof which are used to designate belonging to a particular organizational unit or performing certain functions or services. The above regulation leads to the conclusion that the uniform is a costume which denotes the membership of specific persons to the currently functioning and carrying out specific tasks of organizational units and services, and therefore there is a need to distinguish this type of clothing from clothes intended to commemorate and honor the activities of persons belonging to the Veterans' Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland. The proposed amendment of the law will allow the Minister of National Defence to determine the uniform pattern of the members of the Veterans Corps Walk for Independence of the Republic of Poland. The provisions implementing the Act will also specify the circumstances and manner in which uniforms and elements of uniforms are to be worn. If 70% pensions are not sufficient to cover these costs, the rest is to be covered by the immediate family. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u435	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 31 January 1920, after 148 years of the acquisition of Prussian Chojnice, they were within the borders of reborn Poland. This fact was a consequence of the signing on 28 June 1919 of the Treaty of Versailles by Germany, the States of the Entents and their allies. As a result of the provisions of the main peace act of the First World War, which was the Treaty of Versailles, the city of Chojnice and the railway junction was granted to Poland. Shortly after the signing of this act in Chojnice, there were associations bringing together Poles who tried to mark the fact of regaining independence and joining the city to Poland. The Polish Red Cross branch established by Jan Łukowicz Jr., among others, should be distinguished here. organized a nursing course and initiated the so-called uprising. The soldier's inn. The activity of the Polish inhabitants of Chojnice was visible on various levels, also political within the framework of Polish district delegates at the landsrats and over Mayors, whose aim was to take over the local government from German hands. On January 31, 1920, Colonel Stanisław Wrzaliński led by soldiers 59. Wielkopolska Infantry regiment at 3 p.m. 13 entered Chojnice. Polish soldiers were greeted by the Chojnicki governor Stanisław Sikorski and the mayor of Dr. Alojza Sobierajczyk. I accept the city of Chojnice as a part of the Polish land and, will give God, will remain it forever - in such words, on behalf of the state authorities, Colonel Wrzaliński welcomed the city authorities and inhabitants of Chojnice, solemnly assumed in triumphal gates and white and red colours. The symbolic dimension was the gesture of the young Chojniczanka Bronisława Stammowa, who played the role of Polonia on the balcony, breaking the chains and chains of the Prussian captivity. The Germans did not forget this act, Bronisław Stammov was shot in 1939. There was great joy on that day among Poles - inhabitants of Chojnice, the city which was the most organized city in Pomerania. In the evening of January 31st, a group of Polish soldiers removed the statue of Emperor Wilhelm I located on one of the main squares of the city, which met with protest of the German population. On February 1, the Poles formally took over the city. Commissioner Jan Kalet introduced the Mayor of Dr Alojze Sobierajczyk during the solemn session of the City Council. On February 3, General Józef Dowbor Muśnicki, commander of the Greater Poland Uprising, the Wielkopolska Army and the Wielkopolska Front came to Chojnice. The general was welcomed with due honor by the military, local authorities and residents of Chojnice. On the eve of the regaining independence of Chojnice as a native of Chojniczanin I pay tribute to architects of Polish independence: Ignace Paderewski and Roman Dmowski, who signed the Treaty of Versailles restoring Chojnice Poland on behalf of the Republic of Poland. I pay tribute to all those inhabitants of Chojnice who, after 150 years of captivity in the Prussian partition, contributed to the development of Chojnice and the Second Republic in the interwar years. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u436	Thank you very much. Mr Krzysztof Głuchowski, Law and Justice have the floor.

The 500+ programme, adopted by the High House and implemented, enjoys quite a lot of support in society, reduces poverty, supports large families. It is also intended to encourage young people to stay in the country and start families. It's a noble purpose. On the other hand, it was outrageous to me that Auchan carried out a survey of employees in these large-area shops, in which every employee is to voluntarily enter or use the 500+ program. It's supposed to be casual, but knowing the bad relationship between the world of employers and the world of work, I know there's a restriction behind it. And that is true, because, as it turns out, people who collect the benefit of 500+ will not be able to obtain support from the welfare fund and, if already, in a significantly limited way. I would like to assure the employees of Auchan's large-area shops that not only me, but also my colleagues from the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice do not allow this kind of practice. We are solidifying with you, ladies and gentlemen, to fight for your workers' rights so that you are not discriminated against for being beneficiaries of the 500+ programme. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-25-sejm-34-3.u441	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! All over Poland we feel the severe effects of smog. The current situation significantly worsens the living conditions of all of us. The media alarms almost every day about the catastrophic air condition in Małopolska. In Krakow, the additional costs incurred by health care services due to excessive concentrations of dust reach up to PLN 700 million per year. In a special way, this problem affects my hometown of Rocks. The concentration of dust in the air here is comparable to the most polluted cities in the world. Meanwhile, this town is picturesquely located next to the Ojców National Park and has huge tourist potential. At the last meeting of the Economic Committee of the Council of Ministers, 14 recommendations were made in the fight for better air quality. The standards for boilers in which we smoke have changed. From 2018 onwards it will no longer be possible to install boilers of lower generation than the fifth. Unsorted and low quality coal is to be removed from the sale. The funding from the National Environmental Fund will be given priority to improving air quality. That's about PLN 10 billion. The Ministry of Energy is working on offering a lower night electricity tariff. Thanks to it, people unable to heat their homes with electricity will be able to heat their homes. The Economic Committee of the Council of Ministers also recommends regulations which will block the construction of aeration wedges of cities. All of this can lead to improved air quality. However, for the fight against smog to be effective, administration needs to be mobilised at every level. Self-governments have a particularly important role to play in sensitising Poles to the problem of smog. The local authorities, irrespective of the activities of the central authorities, may inform about the effects of burning in kilns. I strongly encourage you to organise educational meetings where you can learn how to smoke coal in an efficient and environmentally friendly way. Municipalities can also create or expand their low-emission reduction programmes and should also be widely informed about them. It happens that residents do not know about the existence and functioning of such programmes. Without our joint effort, the problem will not go away. Only together with residents can we effectively improve air quality in Poland.
